[{"id":51831,"date":"2026-04-16T15:02:46","date_gmt":"2026-04-16T19:02:46","guid":{"rendered":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/?p=51831"},"modified":"2026-04-23T08:37:07","modified_gmt":"2026-04-23T12:37:07","slug":"arc-seeking-applicants-for-leadership-development-program-focused-on-multi-sector-economic-development-2","status":"publish","type":"post","link":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/news\/arc-seeking-applicants-for-leadership-development-program-focused-on-multi-sector-economic-development-2\/","title":{"rendered":"ARC\u00a0Seeking Applicants for\u00a0Leadership\u00a0Development Program Focused on Multi-Sector Economic Development"},"content":{"rendered":"
\n

Public, private and nonprofit leaders throughout Appalachia are encouraged to apply for ARC\u2019s free eight-month training program.<\/em> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n

\n

WASHINGTON, D.C.,\u00a0April\u00a016, 2026<\/strong>\u00a0\u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) opened applications for the\u00a0eighth\u00a0class of the Appalachian Leadership Institute (ALI),\u00a0an eight month leadership development training opportunity with a focus on economic development in Appalachia. Anyone living or working in Appalachian’s 423 counties is eligible to apply.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n

\n

The program, at no cost to participants, focuses on skill-building, mentoring and collaboration to grow Appalachia’s economic feature. The curriculum comprises six multi-day seminars across Appalachia, each focusing on one of ARC’s investment priorities, and concludes with a graduation ceremony in Binghamton, New York.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n

\n

From October 2026 to June 2027, the eighth cohort of fellows will learn to: <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n

\n
    \n
  • Recognize and utilize unique assets in their communities to build economic development plans. <\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n
    \n
      \n
    • Identify and implement strategies to strengthen their communities through improved infrastructure, business development, workforce development, tourism and community skill-building. <\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n
      \n
        \n
      • Collaborate with a\u202fnetwork of leaders across Appalachia on strategies to expand the region\u2019s future growth. <\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n
        \n

        \u201cBy bringing together changemakers from across our 13 states, ARC\u2019s Appalachian Leadership Institute not only invests in the future of our Appalachian economy, but also in the future of our Appalachian people,\u201d said ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin. <\/strong>\u201cI\u2019m confident the members of our eighth cohort will continue the institute\u2019s work of learning and sharing by using collaboration as a tool to help our region reach its full economic potential, and I look forward to seeing the applications we receive.\u201d <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n

        \n

        The Appalachian Leadership Institute is limited to 40 fellows each year and includes participants from each of the 13 Appalachian states. Applicants may be from the public, private or non-profit sectors, and each class represents a wide network of experiences.  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n

        \n

        “We’ve made it clear in Mississippi and the Appalachian Region that we are going to compete and win when it comes to economic growth and opportunity. That takes leadership,\u201d said ARC 2026 States\u2019 Co-Chair, Mississippi Governor Tate Reeves<\/strong>. \u201cThe Appalachian Leadership Institute is helping build that leadership pipeline across our state and region. I encourage Appalachians who are ready to step up and make an impact to apply and be part of that effort.” <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n

        \n

        Upon completion of the program, fellows automatically join the ALI Alumni Network, a peer-to-peer working group committed to collaborative solutions that strengthen Appalachia. The alumni network provides an opportunity to stay connected to the network of more than 200 ALI alumni and to continue learning from leaders in Appalachian economic development. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n

        \n

        Applications for the 2026-2027 class are open through June 1, 2026. To learn more about the program and apply, visit: arc.gov\/leadership<\/a>.  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n

        \n

        ### <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n

        \n

        About the Appalachian Leadership Institute<\/strong>
        The Appalachian Leadership Institute is a comprehensive regional leadership training program developed by the Appalachian Regional Commission in partnership with the University of Tennessee, Knoxville; Tuskegee University; and Collective Impact. The current class of the Appalachian Leadership Institute includes representatives from a variety of sectors and industries such as education, healthcare, economic development, public service, and other professions.\u00a0<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n

        \n

        About the Appalachian Regional Commission<\/strong> 
        The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to invest and partner to strengthen economic development in Appalachia to help the region reach its full economic potential. <\/p>\n<\/div>","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"

Announces Appalachian Leadership Institute Class of 2025-2026\u00a0"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong><em>New class of ARC\u2019s economic development training program comprises 40 fellows from across all 13 Appalachian states.<\/em><\/strong> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>WASHINGTON, D.C., October 23, 2025<\/strong>\u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) announced the 40 fellows selected for the 2025-2026 class of the <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/leadership\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Appalachian Leadership Institute<\/a> (ALI), a training opportunity that equips <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/about-the-appalachian-region\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Appalachian<\/a> community leaders to use economic development as a tool to drive positive change. The seventh ALI class comprises a network of public, private and nonprofit leaders from all 13 Appalachian states. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The eight-month program, which kicked off with an orientation session in Ashland, KY, this week, is anchored by six multi-day seminars across the Appalachian Region. Each seminar focuses on one of ARC\u2019s <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/investment-priorities\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">strategic investment priorities<\/a> and will prepare these fellows to build effective economic and community development strategies, integrate their local assets into wider regional development strategies and use new connections to forge strong regional partnerships. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cInvesting in Appalachia\u2019s local leaders is an integral part of maintaining a strong economy for our region,\u201d said <strong>ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin<\/strong>. \u201cI\u2019m proud of the way ARC\u2019s Appalachian Leadership Institute capitalizes on the talent already within our 13 states and empowers fellows to connect and collaborate on projects that will impact their communities and Appalachia as a region.\u201d <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/academies-and-institutes\/leadership\/class-of-2025-2026\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">2025-2026 Class of ALI fellows<\/a> were selected via a competitive application process to reflect the region\u2019s wide range of economic development challenges, opportunities and strategies. The class includes: <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li><strong>Alabama<\/strong><div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>Christy Atkinson, Executive Director, Workshops Empowerment, Inc., Pell City <\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Jori Chatman, Program Manager of Government & Community Relations, Tennessee Valley Authority, Florence <\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Mary-Marshall VanSant, Director of Center for Learning & Professional Development, University of North Alabama, Tuscumbia <\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/div><\/li>\n\n\n\n<li><strong>Georgia<\/strong><div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>Jessica Dudley, President, United Way of Hall County, Lula <\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Noel Durant, AVP Tennessee State Director, Trust for Public Land, Chattanooga <\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Alexander Harrison, Director of Parks & Recreation, Catoosa County GA, Ringgold<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/div><\/li>\n\n\n\n<li><strong>Kentucky<\/strong><div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>Rachel Chambers, Supervisory Community class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cIn Maryland, economic growth is our North Star,\u201d said <strong>ARC 2025 States\u2019 Co-Chair, Maryland Governor Wes Moore<\/strong>. \u201cOur ability to grow the economy across Appalachia depends on not just clear investments from the government, but also on grassroots leadership within our communities. I congratulate each of the 40 fellows selected for the 2025-2026 class and look forward to watching their success unfold throughout our region.\u201d <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Upon completion of the program, fellows will continue to build and share knowledge with hundreds of other leaders in the Appalachian Leadership Institute Alumni Network, a peer working group fostering collaborative solutions to build a stronger economic future for Appalachia. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">To learn more about the program, visit: <a href=\"http:\/\/arc.gov\/leadership\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">arc.gov\/leadership<\/a>.  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>About the Appalachian Leadership Institute<\/em>   <br>The Appalachian Leadership Institute is a comprehensive economic development training program for regional leaders developed by the Appalachian Regional Commission in partnership with the University of Tennessee, Knoxville; Tuskegee University; and Collective Impact, LLC. Learn more at www.arc.gov\/leadership.   <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>About the Appalachian Regional Commission  <\/em> <br>The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the region achieve socioeconomic parity with the .org\/{rel}","templated":true}]}},{"id":50050,"date":"2025-09-17T11:07:45","date_gmt":"2025-09-17T15:07:45","guid":{"rendered":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/?p=50050"},"modified":"2025-09-30T07:34:37","modified_gmt":"2025-09-30T11:34:37","slug":"arc-awards-11-million-to-32-projects-supporting-appalachians-in-recovery-from-substance-use-disorder","status":"publish","type":"post","link":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/news\/arc-awards-11-million-to-32-projects-supporting-appalachians-in-recovery-from-substance-use-disorder\/","title":{"rendered":"ARC Awards $11 Million to 32 Projects Supporting Appalachians in Recovery from Substance Use Disorder\u00a0"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>Through ARC\u2019s INSPIRE Initiative, the award package will expand support services to help Appalachians in recovery enter and re-enter the workforce in nine Appalachian states. <\/em> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>WASHINGTON, DC, September 23, 2025<\/strong>\u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) awarded $11 million to 32 projects through its<strong>\u202f<a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/grants-and-opportunities\/sud\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Investments Supporting Partnerships in Recovery Ecosystems (INSPIRE)<\/a><\/strong>\u202fInitiative, which aims to address workforce gaps and economic challenges due to the impact of substance use disorder (SUD) in Appalachia.   <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2025\/09\/INSPIRE-Award-Summaries-by-State-September-2025.pdf\">ARC\u2019s 2025 INSPIRE grantees<\/a><\/strong> will strengthen the SUD recovery ecosystem in 165 counties across nine Appalachian states\u2014 Georgia, Kentucky, New York, North Carolina, Ohio, Pennsylvania, Tennessee, Virginia and West Virginia \u2014by providing community support to help individuals in SUD recovery enter or re-enter the workforce while maintaining their recovery. Twenty of the impacted counties are first-time INSPIRE award recipients.  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cIn order to have a healthy Appalachian economy, we must make sure we have a healthy Appalachian workforce,\u201d said <strong>ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin<\/strong>. \u201cI am so proud of the way ARC\u2019s INSPIRE initiative ecosystem empowers Appalachians in substance use disorder recovery to gain the training, resources and support they need to enter or re-enter the workforce while also becoming productive members of their families and communities.\u201d <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">“Everyone knows someone affected by substance use. It will take all of us working together to get those in recovery back on their feet,” said <strong>ARC 2025 States’ Co-Chair, Maryland Governor Wes Moore.<\/strong> “Today’s announcement accelerates our work to help those in need, while also building fresh pathwas to work, wages and wealth for all. This is good news for public health, good news for economic growth, and good news for mounting prosperity across the region.”<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Since April 2021, ARC\u2019s INSPIRE Initiative has invested $65.7 million in 200 projects that have impacted 380 counties across all 13 Appalachian states. These investments have secured an additional $48.1 million in matching project funds and are projected to improve nearly 4,000 businesses and help prepare over 18,100 individuals for new job opportunities. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong><em>New Data Reveals Reduction in SUD-related Deaths in Appalachia<\/em><\/strong> <br>SUD recovery-to-work initiatives may be starting to make an impact on overdose mortality, according to a <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/report\/appalachian-diseases-of-despair-2025\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">new ARC research report<\/a> that examines trends in diseases of despair including overdose, suicide, and liver disease. From 2021 to 2023, the overdose mortality rate decreased 10 percent in the Appalachian Region. However, work remains to be done, as these rates\u2014in both Appalachia and the rest of the country\u2014remain much higher than the pre-pandemic figures of 2019. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Learn more about ARC\u2019s INSPIRE Initiative and our new INSPIRE grantees at\u202f<a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/grants-and-opportunities\/sud\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">arc.gov\/SUD<\/a>. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>About the Appalachian Regional Commission<\/strong> <br>The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation. <\/p>\n<\/div>","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"<p>Through ARC\u2019s INSPIRE Initiative, the award package will expand support services to help Appalachians in recovery enter and its\u202fInvestments Supporting Partnerships in Recovery Ecosystems (INSPIRE)\u202fInitiative, which aims to address workforce gaps Announces Appalachian Leadership Institute Class of 2024-2025\u00a0"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong><em>Sixth class of ARC\u2019s nine-month economic development training program comprises 40 fellows from the public, private, and nonprofit sectors across all 13 Appalachian states.<\/em><\/strong><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>WASHINGTON, D.C., October 23, 2024<\/strong>\u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) announced the 40 fellows selected for the 2024-2025 Class of the <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/leadership\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Appalachian Leadership Institute<\/a> (ALI), a leadership training opportunity that equips <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/about-the-appalachian-region\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Appalachian<\/a> community leaders to use economic development as a tool to drive positive change. The sixth ALI class comprises a diverse network of public, private, and nonprofit leaders from all 13 Appalachian states.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The nine-month curriculum, which kicked off with an orientation session in Knoxville, TN this week, is anchored by six multi-day seminars across the Appalachian Region. Each seminar focuses on one of ARC\u2019s <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/investment-priorities\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">strategic investment priorities<\/a> and includes skill-building workshops, best practice analysis, networking, and site visits to see ARC <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/investments-in-action\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">investments in action<\/a>.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cThe Appalachian Leadership Institute supports the enduring work of Appalachia\u2019s changemakers, while helping them advance their skillsets and build upon<strong> <\/strong>valuable regional connections with likeminded leaders,\u201d said <strong>ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin<\/strong>. \u201cI send my sincerest congratulations to our newest round of ALI fellows knowing their passion and purpose will ensure an even more vibrant future for Appalachia\u2019s communities.\u201d<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/academies-and-institutes\/leadership\/class-of-2024-2025\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">2024-2025 Class of ALI fellows<\/a> \u2013 who were selected via a competitive application process to reflect the Appalachian region\u2019s wide range of economic development challenges, opportunities, and strategies \u2013 includes:<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li><strong>Alabama<\/strong><div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li><strong>Ryan regional development strategies, identify public grants and other community development resources, and use new connections to forge strong regional partnerships.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cARC\u2019s Appalachian Leadership Institute is a valuable program that allows participants to learn more about each of ARC\u2019s strategic investment priorities and the needs of the region,\u201d said <strong>ARC States\u2019 Co-Chair Tennessee Governor Bill Lee.<\/strong> \u201cI\u2019m proud of the three Tennesseans chosen to participate in this class and look forward to seeing how they’ll use their leadership skills to engage in their communities.\u201d<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Upon completion of the program, fellows will continue to build and share knowledge with hundreds of other leaders in the Appalachian Leadership Institute Alumni Network, a peer working group fostering collaborative solutions to build a stronger future for Appalachia.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">To learn more about the program, visit: <a href=\"http:\/\/arc.gov\/leadership\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">arc.gov\/leadership<\/a>. To meet the 2024-2025 class of fellows and learn more about their work to strengthen Appalachian communities, visit: <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/academies-and-institutes\/leadership\/class-of-2024-2025\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/academies-and-institutes\/leadership\/class-of-2024-2025\/<\/a>.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>About the Appalachian Leadership Institute<\/em>   <br>The Appalachian Leadership Institute is a comprehensive regional leadership training program developed by the Appalachian Regional Commission in partnership with the University of Tennessee, Knoxville; The Howard H. Baker School of Public Policy and Public Affairs; Tuskegee University; and Collective Impact. Learn more at www.arc.gov\/leadership.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>About the Appalachian Regional Commission  <\/em> <br>The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the Region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation.<\/p>\n<\/div>","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"<p>Sixth class of ARC\u2019s nine-month economic development training program comprises 40 fellows from the public, private, and nonprofit sectors across all 13 Appalachian states. Awards $68.2 Million for Economic Revitalization in Appalachia\u2019s Coal-Impacted Communities"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>Largest POWER funding package to date is expected to create more than 2,400 new jobs and train over 10,500 workers for new opportunities in Appalachia\u2019s coal-impacted communities across 10 states<\/em>.<br><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>PITTSBURGH, Pa., October 16, 2024<\/strong>\u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) awarded <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2024\/10\/POWER-Award-Summaries-October-2024.pdf\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">$68.2 million to 65 projects<\/a> through its <a href=\"http:\/\/arc.gov\/POWER\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Partnerships for Opportunity and Workforce and Economic Revitalization (POWER) Initiative<\/a>. POWER directs federal resources to economic diversification and revitalization projects in Appalachian communities affected by the downturn of the coal industry.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Today\u2019s award package is ARC’s largest POWER investment since the initiative was launched in 2015, with projects impacting 188 counties in 10 Appalachian states: Alabama, Kentucky, Mississippi, New York, North Carolina, Ohio, Pennsylvania, Tennessee, Virginia and West Virginia. ARC recognizes that Hurricane Helene has impacted multiple Appalachian communities and grantees, including some POWER award recipients. ARC will continue working with affected state and grantee partners to address their needs as they recover and rebuild.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cARC\u2019s POWER initiative supports coal-impacted communities’ preparation for the next phase of Appalachia\u2019s economy, while ensuring that residents have a say in the course of their own futures,\u201d said <strong>ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin<\/strong>. \u201cThe investments announced in this round of POWER will help train workers, advance new industries, and build upon the progress already being made toward a brighter future full of economic opportunity for our region.\u201d <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">ARC\u2019s 2024 POWER awardees and their partners will use funding to strengthen a variety of industries \u2013 including advanced manufacturing, entrepreneurship, healthcare, and workforce development \u2013 to enhance job training and employment opportunities, create jobs in existing and new industries, and attract new sources of private investment in coal-impacted communities. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin announced the awards alongside state and federal partners and grantees at the Mill 19 in Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania, headquarters of POWER grantee <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/investment\/advancing-new-industries-in-northern-appalachia\/\">Catalyst Connection<\/a> and sub-grantees Carnegie Mellon University\u2019s Manufacturing Futures Institute and the Advanced Robotics for Manufacturing (ARM) Institute. Catalyst Connection\u2019s CEO Petra Mitchell spoke about the new $2 million POWER grant to help create pathways to employment in advanced manufacturing.\u00a0<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cWe are excited for our new POWER project, which will allow us to build on our progress of preparing our regional workforce for in-demand jobs in the advanced manufacturing industry,” said <strong>Catalyst Connection President and CEO Petra Mitchell.<\/strong> “Manufacturing jobs offer workers from hard hit Appalachian communities hope and opportunities for family-sustaining wages and community development. When manufacturing companies and workers succeed, everyone in the local community can benefit.”<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Since 2015, ARC has invested $484.7 million in 564 projects impacting 365 coal-impacted counties. Collectively, these investments are projected to support nearly 54,000 jobs and prepare nearly 170,000 workers and students for new opportunities in growing industries. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">ARC plans to release a notice of solicitations of applications (NOSA) for the POWER Initiative in early 2025.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Learn more about ARC\u2019s POWER Initiative and our new POWER grantees at <a href=\"http:\/\/arc.gov\/POWER\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">arc.gov\/POWER<\/a>.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>About the Appalachian Regional Commission<\/em><br>The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n\n<!-- \/templates\/blocks\/content-related-content.twig -->\n\n\n\t\n\n\n\n<div class=\"feature-cards feature-cards--\">\n <div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--medium\">\n v\/wp-json\/wp\/v2\/operational_guidance_type?post=45958"}],"curies":[{"name":"wp","href":"https:\/\/api.w.org\/{rel}","templated":true}]}},{"id":45434,"date":"2024-09-17T09:58:09","date_gmt":"2024-09-17T13:58:09","guid":{"rendered":"https:\/\/arc.gov\/?p=45434"},"modified":"2025-07-25T08:03:37","modified_gmt":"2025-07-25T12:03:37","slug":"arc-awards-nearly-11-5-million-to-39-projects-supporting-appalachians-in-recovery-from-substance-use-disorder","status":"publish","type":"post","link":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/news\/arc-awards-nearly-11-5-million-to-39-projects-supporting-appalachians-in-recovery-from-substance-use-disorder\/","title":{"rendered":"ARC Awards Nearly $11.5 Million to 39 Projects Supporting Appalachians in Recovery from Substance Use Disorder"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>Through ARC\u2019s INSPIRE Initiative, the award package will expand support services to help Appalachians in recovery enter and re-enter the workforce in nine Appalachian states.<\/em><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Asheville, N.C., September 18, 2024<\/strong>\u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) awarded nearly <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2024\/09\/INSPIRE-Award-Summaries-by-State-September-2024.pdf\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">$11.5 million to 39 projects<\/a> through its <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/grants-and-opportunities\/sud\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Investments Supporting Partnerships in Recovery Ecosystems (INSPIRE)<\/a> Initiative, which aims to address the impact of substance use disorder (SUD) in Appalachia with investments in projects that create or expand services in the recovery ecosystem leading to workforce entry and re-entry.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2024\/09\/INSPIRE-Award-Summaries-by-State-September-2024.pdf\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">ARC\u2019s 2024 INSPIRE grantees<\/a> will strengthen the SUD recovery ecosystem in 127 counties in nine Appalachian states\u2014Kentucky, New York, North Carolina, Ohio, Pennsylvania, South Carolina, Tennessee, Virginia, and West Virginia\u2014by expanding recovery-focused partnerships, peer support and other wraparound services, and skills and workforce training programs that help prepare individuals in recovery for meaningful job opportunities.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cSubstance use disorder is a region-wide epidemic that impacts Appalachian families and community workforces,\u201d said <strong>ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin<\/strong>. \u201cI commend our 2024 INSPIRE grantees for their dedication in helping Appalachians who have struggled with substance use disorder regain a sense of hope and purpose by enabling them to rejoin their communities, bolster workforce development, and make positive impacts on the region.\u201d<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">“In order to truly address substance use disorder, we must create an environment that includes support for wrap-around services such as housing, job training, and counseling,” said <strong>ARC States’ Co-Chair Tennessee Governor Bill Lee.<\/strong> “INSPIRE funding helps make this possible by empowering organizations to make an impact across the Appalachian region. We are proud of Tennessee’s nine grantees who are focused on this important work.”<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Federal Co-Chair Manchin made the award announcement with <a href=\"https:\/\/www.ncdhhs.gov\/about\/leadership\/kody-kinsley\">North Carolina Health and Human Services Secretary Kody Kinsley<\/a>, grantees, and state partners during a press conference at the headquarters of <a href=\"https:\/\/arc.gov\/investment\/land-of-sky-regional-council-expanding-recovery-to-work-support-services-in-western-north-carolina\/\">Land of Sky Regional Council<\/a>\u2014INSPIRE grantee and local development district\u2014in Asheville, North Carolina.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cCommunities in the Appalachian region, especially rural towns, have borne the brunt of the disease of addiction,\u201d said <strong>Secretary Kinsley<\/strong>. \u201cWe are grateful to our federal partners for tackling these disparities head-on by investing in behavioral health services and supports.\u201d<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">With this award package, ARC has invested $53.6 million in 166 projects across all 13 Appalachian states since INSPIRE was established in April of 2021. Together, ARC\u2019s INSPIRE investments will impact 360 Appalachian counties, improve over 3,000 businesses and help prepare nearly 16,200 individuals for new opportunities in the workforce.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>New Data Reveals Reduction in SUD-related Deaths in Appalachia<\/em><br>SUD recovery-to-work initiatives may be starting to make an impact, according to a <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/report\/appalachian-diseases-of-despair-2024\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">new ARC research report<\/a> that examines trends in diseases of despair including overdose, suicide, and liver disease. From 2021 to 2022, the overall diseases of despair mortality rate decreased 4 percent in the Appalachian Region while remaining virtually unchanged in the rest of the United States. However, work remains to be done, as these rates\u2014in both Appalachia and the rest of the country\u2014remain much higher than the pre-pandemic figures of 2019.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">ARC expects to issue a Notice of Solicitation for Applications (NOSA) for the next round of INSPIRE funding in early 2025.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Learn more about ARC’s INSPIRE Initiative and our new INSPIRE grantees at <a href=\"http:\/\/arc.gov\/SUD\">arc.gov\/SUD<\/a>.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>About the Appalachian Regional Commission<\/em><br>The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n\n<!-- \/templates\/blocks\/content-related-content.twig -->\n\n\n\t\n\n\n\n<div class=\"feature-cards feature-cards--\">\n <div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--medium\">\n Administration Through ARC Awards $16.9 Million to Grow Appalachia\u2019s Infrastructure, Businesses, Workforce and Tourism Across 10 States"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>Five new Appalachian Regional Commission ARISE grants drive multi-state collaboration to transform the regional economy.<\/em><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Chattanooga, Tenn., September 5, 2024<\/strong>\u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) announced $16.9 million in funding for five collaborative projects to expand the region\u2019s infrastructure, tourism, business development and workforce opportunities. Funded through ARC\u2019s <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/grants-and-opportunities\/arise\/\">Appalachian Regional Initiative for Stronger Economies (ARISE)<\/a>, these projects bring together more than 60 partners representing at least 270 counties across 10 Appalachian states\u2014including one of Appalachia\u2019s federally recognized <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/tribal-communities-in-the-appalachian-region\/\">Tribal Communities<\/a>\u2014to drive large-scale economic transformation. ARISE grantees announced today include:<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li><strong><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2024\/08\/2024.09-ARISE-Project-Snapshot-Save-the-Children-Federation-Inc.pdf\">$8,000,000 to Save the Children Federation, Inc.<\/a><\/strong> to grow the region\u2019s childcare and early education workforce in Kentucky, Tennessee and West Virginia through training, credentialing, apprenticeships and technical assistance.<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li><strong><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2024\/08\/2024.09-ARISE-Project-Snapshot-Beneficial-Electrification-League.pdf\">$4,500,000 to the Beneficial Electrification League<\/a><\/strong> to modernize electric utility grid infrastructure in Alabama, Georgia, Mississippi, North Carolina, New York, Ohio, Pennsylvania and Tennessee.<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li><strong><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2024\/08\/2024.09-ARISE-Project-Snapshot-Appalachian-Investors-Alliance.pdf\">$3,889,964 to the Appalachian Investors Alliance<\/a><\/strong> to establish micro-venture and angel funds for entrepreneurs and small businesses, and launch the Appalachian Venture Foundry to prepare businesses for investments, in Kentucky, Mississippi, Ohio, Pennsylvania, Tennessee and West Virginia.<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li><strong><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2024\/08\/2024.09-ARISE-Project-Snapshot-Carnegie-Mellon-University.pdf\">$500,000 to Carnegie Mellon University<\/a><\/strong> to plan for the expansion of job skills matching and career support services in Pennsylvania and West Virginia.<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li><strong><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2024\/08\/2024.09-ARISE-Project-Snapshot-Pennsylvania-Environmental-Council.pdf\">$72,219 to the Pennsylvania Environmental Council<\/a><\/strong> to plan for improved trail connectivity to boost tourism in Pennsylvania and New York, including the Seneca Nation of Indians.<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin announced the awards during ARC\u2019s <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/event\/arc-2024-annual-conference\/\">2024 Annual Conference<\/a> in Chattanooga, Tennessee. She was joined by ARC\u2019s 2024 States\u2019 Co-Chair Governor Bill Lee and hundreds of federal, state and community leaders from across the 13-state Appalachian Region.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cBy combining multi-state collaboration with innovative partnerships, ARC\u2019s ARISE projects serve as models for how we can work as one, united Appalachia to maximize economic growth,\u201d said <strong>ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin<\/strong>. \u201cI congratulate our newest ARISE grantees and am proud to have announced them in the presence of many of our federal, state, and local partners at ARC\u2019s annual conference. I have no doubt that these ARISE projects\u2014which include both planning and implementation grants\u2014will build on previous successes and result in greater opportunities for our Appalachian people and communities.\u201d<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">With today\u2019s package, ARC has invested <strong>$88.2 million<\/strong> in <strong>34 ARISE projects<\/strong> to support the development of new economic opportunities <strong>across all 13 Appalachian states<\/strong>.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Made possible through support from the Bipartisan Infrastructure Law, ARISE drives large-scale, regional economic transformation through collaborative multi-state projects.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">To learn more about the new ARISE grantees, visit <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/grants-and-opportunities\/arise\/arise-project-summaries\/\">www.arc.gov\/grants-and-opportunities\/arise\/arise-project-summaries\/<\/a>.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>About the Appalachian Regional Commission<\/em><br>The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation.<\/p>\n<\/div>","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"<p>Five new Appalachian Regional Commission ARISE grants drive multi-state collaboration to transform the regional economy. Regional Commission Selects 30 Community Foundations for No-Cost Training Program and Funding Opportunity"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>READY Community Foundations will provide 30 community foundations serving the Appalachian Region with no-cost training and access to funding to expand local philanthropic impact.<\/em><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>WASHINGTON, D.C., August 29, 2024<\/strong> \u2013 Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) announced the 30 community foundations, serving communities in 10 Appalachian states, selected to participate in <em><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/ready\/foundations\/\">READY Community Foundations<\/a><\/em>. One of four tailored tracks under ARC\u2019s capacity-building initiative, <em><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/ready\/\">READY Appalachia<\/a><\/em>, <em>READY Community Foundations<\/em> provides no-cost training and funding access to help Appalachia\u2019s local philanthropic organizations improve their programming, operations, fundraising and financial impact.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2024\/08\/2024.08-READY-CF-Participants-Final.png\">30 organizations<\/a> selected for <em>READY Community Foundations <\/em>will participate in six weeks of virtual, cohort-based courses designed to improve their skills in financial management, endowment management, community-rooted fundraising and grantmaking, mission and strategy development and more.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cARC remains committed to providing our Appalachian communities with the resources they need to build toward a prosperous future, including training to strengthen community foundation capacity,\u201d said <strong>ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin<\/strong>. \u201cThrough the training provided by <em>READY Community Foundations<\/em>, Appalachia\u2019s philanthropic organizations will be better able to respond to community needs and foster economic development in the areas they serve.\u201d<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Following completion of the training program, participants will be eligible to apply for up to $25,000 in funding (no match required) to implement internal capacity-building projects that will help them better serve Appalachian Kentucky, Maryland, Mississippi, New York, North Carolina, Ohio, Pennsylvania, Tennessee, Virginia, and West Virginia.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">All of the <em>READY Community Foundations <\/em>participants serve at least one of the following:<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>Distressed areas and census tracts<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Interagency Working Group on Coal and Power Plant Communities <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2022\/08\/IWG-Appalachian-Counties.pdf\">priority communities<\/a><\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Rural Partners Network <a href=\"https:\/\/www.rural.gov\/community-networks\">Community Networks communities<\/a><\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Historically underserved populations<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Supported with funding from the Bipartisan Infrastructure Law, <em>READY Community Foundations<\/em> in the fourth and final track under our capacity-building initiative, READY Appalachia. Since the launch of READY Appalachia in July 2022, ARC has provided training and access to funding to 41 <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/map\/local-development-districts\/\">local development districts<\/a> (LDDs), 75 nonprofits, and 90 <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/ready\/localgovs\/\">local government<\/a> entities to help them build internal capacity and better serve their communities. To date, nearly $4 million has been awarded through <em><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/ready\/ldds\/\">READY Local Development Districts<\/a><\/em> and more than $1.6 million has been awarded through <em><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/ready\/nonprofits\/\">READY Nonprofits<\/a><\/em>.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cToday\u2019s <em>READY Community Foundations<\/em> announcement is yet another example of how the Biden-Harris Administration\u2019s Bipartisan Infrastructure Law is revitalizing the foundations of local communities across America \u2013 including energy communities that have historically been left behind in Appalachia,\u201d said<strong> National Economic Advisor Lael Brainard.<\/strong><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">To learn more about the 30 <em>READY Community Foundations<\/em> participants, visit <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/ready\/foundations\">https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/ready\/foundations\/<\/a>.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>About the Appalachian Regional Commission<\/em><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>About READY Appalachia<\/em><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>READY Appalachia<\/em> is an ARC community-capacity building initiative for nonprofit organizations, local development districts (LDDs), local governments, and community foundations serving the Appalachian Region. Participants access no-cost training, cohort-based learning, and funding opportunities (no match required) to increase their ability to solve pressing issues and create lasting, positive change in communities.<\/p>\n<\/div>","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"<p>READY Community Foundations will provide 30 community foundations serving the Appalachian Region with no-cost training and access to funding to expand local philanthropic impact. WASHINGTON, D.C., August 29, 2024 \u2013 Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) announced the 30 community foundations, serving communities in 10 Appalachian states, selected to participate in READY Community Foundations. Administration Designates Nine Tech Hubs and Grant Recipients in Appalachian Region"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em><strong>As part of the President\u2019s Investing in America agenda, the U.S. Department of Commerce identified regional centers primed for technological innovation and job creation.<\/strong><\/em><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">WASHINGTON, DC \u2014 The Biden-Harris administration, through the U.S. Department of Commerce\u2019s Economic Development Administration (EDA), <a href=\"https:\/\/www.eda.gov\/news\/press-release\/2023\/10\/23\/biden-harris-administration-designates-31-tech-hubs-across-america\">announced<\/a> the designation of 31 Tech Hubs in regions across the country\u2014three of which are in in the Appalachian Region. EDA also awarded six Strategy Development Grants (SDG) to Appalachian entities to help communities significantly increase local coordination and planning activities. Such development could make selected grantees more competitive for future Tech Hubs funding opportunities.<br><br>This is the first phase of the new <a href=\"https:\/\/www.eda.gov\/funding\/programs\/regional-technology-and-innovation-hubs\">Tech Hubs program<\/a>, which is an economic development initiative designed to drive regional innovation and job creation by strengthening a region\u2019s capacity to manufacture, commercialize, and deploy technology that will advance American competitiveness. The program invests directly in burgeoning, high-potential U.S. regions and aims to transform them into globally competitive innovation centers.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cI\u2019m so appreciative of the EDA for recognizing the skillset of our Appalachian workforce, and for their continuing willingness to keep in mind the priority service areas of their fellow federal entities,\u201d said <strong>ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin<\/strong>. \u201cThe creation of new Appalachian tech hubs not only emphasizes our region\u2019s growth, but also the ways in which we are being connected with the rest of the country through emerging industries.\u201d<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>The Appalachian Tech Hubs are:<\/strong><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ol class=\"wp-block-list\" type=\"1\">\n<li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.eda.gov\/funding\/programs\/regional-technology-and-innovation-hubs\/2023\/Birmingham-Biotechnology-Hub\">Birmingham Biotechnology Hub<\/a> \u2013 equitable AI-driven biotechnology in AL<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.eda.gov\/funding\/programs\/regional-technology-and-innovation-hubs\/2023\/SC-Nexus-for-Advanced-Resilient-Energy\">SC Nexus for Advanced Resilient Energy<\/a> \u2013 clean energy supply chain in SC and GA<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.eda.gov\/funding\/programs\/regional-technology-and-innovation-hubs\/2023\/New-Energy-New-York-Battery-Tech-Hub\">New Energy New York (NENY) Battery Tech Hub<\/a> \u2013 end to end battery development and manufacturing in NY<\/li>\n<\/ol>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>The Appalachian Strategy Development Grant (SDG) Recipients are:<\/strong><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ol class=\"wp-block-list\" type=\"1\">\n<li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.eda.gov\/funding\/programs\/regional-technology-and-innovation-hubs\/2023\/North-Central-Pressed-Materials-Strategy-Development-Consortium\">North Central Pressed Materials Strategy Development Consortium<\/a><\/li>\n\n\n\n<li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.eda.gov\/funding\/programs\/regional-technology-and-innovation-hubs\/2023\/Southeast-Biotech-Collaborative-Strategy-Development-Consortium\">Southeast Biotech Collaborative (SEBC) Strategy Development Consortium<\/a><\/li>\n\n\n\n<li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.eda.gov\/funding\/programs\/regional-technology-and-innovation-hubs\/2023\/Virginias-Additive-Manufacturing-Applied-Materials-Strategy-Development-Consortium\">Virginia’s Additive Manufacturing & Applied Materials Strategy Development Consortium<\/a><\/li>\n\n\n\n<li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.eda.gov\/funding\/programs\/regional-technology-and-innovation-hubs\/2023\/West-Virginia-Advanced-Energy-Industrial-Technology-Manufacturing-Strategy-Development-Consortium\">West Virginia Advanced Energy & Industrial Technology Manufacturing (WV-AEIM) Strategy Development Consortium<\/a><\/li>\n\n\n\n<li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.eda.gov\/funding\/programs\/regional-technology-and-innovation-hubs\/2023\/Western-North-Carolina-Industrialized-Construction-Strategy-Development-Consortium\">Western North Carolina Industrialized Construction (NCIC) Strategy Development Consortium<\/a><\/li>\n\n\n\n<li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.eda.gov\/funding\/programs\/regional-technology-and-innovation-hubs\/2023\/WV-Tech-Strategy-Development-Consortium\">WV Tech Strategy Development Consortium<\/a><\/li>\n<\/ol>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Tech Hubs was authorized by the bipartisan CHIPS and Science Act, a key part of President Biden\u2019s Investing in America agenda, which he signed into law in August 2022.  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">These Tech Hubs represent a cross-section of urban and rural regions. Designation is an endorsement of the region\u2019s plans to supercharge their respective technological industries to create jobs, strengthen U.S. competitiveness, and protect national security. The Tech Hubs announced focus on industries ranging from autonomous systems, quantum computing, biotechnology, precision medicine, clean energy advancement, semiconductor manufacturing, and more, and highlight how the Biden Harris Administration is investing in innovation and economic growth in every region of the United States.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cThese outstanding Tech Hubs Designees exemplify place-based economic development strategies at their best: combining federal resources with regional assets, expertise, and coalitions to implement transformational opportunities, said <strong>Assistant Secretary of Commerce for Economic Development Alejandra Y. Castillo<\/strong>. \u201cAs each region develops its own strategy to catalyze innovation and job creation, the entire nation grows more secure and more competitive.\u201d<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Read more about the Tech Hubs program at <\/strong><a href=\"https:\/\/www.eda.gov\/funding\/programs\/regional-technology-and-innovation-hubs\"><strong>TechHubs.gov<\/strong><\/a>.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Read the full EDA press release here: <a href=\"https:\/\/www.eda.gov\/news\/press-release\/2023\/10\/23\/biden-harris-administration-designates-31-tech-hubs-across-america\">https:\/\/www.eda.gov\/news\/press-release\/2023\/10\/23\/biden-harris-administration-designates-31-tech-hubs-across-america<\/a><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>About the U.S. Economic Development Administration (<\/strong><a href=\"http:\/\/www.eda.gov\/\"><strong>www.eda.gov<\/strong><\/a><strong>)<\/strong><br>The mission of the U.S. Economic Development Administration (EDA) is to lead the federal economic development agenda by promoting competitiveness and preparing the nation\u2019s regions for growth and success in the worldwide economy. An agency within the U.S. Department of Commerce, EDA invests in communities and supports regional collaboration in order to create jobs for U.S. workers, promote American innovation, and accelerate long-term sustainable economic growth.<\/p>\n<\/div>","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"<p>As part of the President\u2019s Investing in America agenda, the U.S. Department of Commerce identified regional centers primed for technological innovation and job creation. across the country\u2014three of which are rc.gov\/wp-json\/wp\/v2\/operational_guidance_type?post=41209"}],"curies":[{"name":"wp","href":"https:\/\/api.w.org\/{rel}","templated":true}]}},{"id":40957,"date":"2023-10-10T08:52:47","date_gmt":"2023-10-10T13:52:47","guid":{"rendered":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/?p=40957"},"modified":"2025-05-27T09:44:49","modified_gmt":"2025-05-27T13:44:49","slug":"biden-harris-administration-through-arc-awards-14-5-million-to-grow-manufacturing-in-green-energy-sectors-workforce-development-opportunities-in-northern-and-central-appalachia","status":"publish","type":"post","link":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/news\/biden-harris-administration-through-arc-awards-14-5-million-to-grow-manufacturing-in-green-energy-sectors-workforce-development-opportunities-in-northern-and-central-appalachia\/","title":{"rendered":"ARC Awards $14.5M+ to Grow Manufacturing in Northern and Central Appalachia"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong><em>Two new ARISE grants strengthen manufacturing and energy production and prepare youth for in-demand jobs across five states.<\/em><\/strong> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>WASHINGTON, D.C., October 10, 2023<\/strong>\u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) awarded two new grants totaling over $14.5 million to boost energy manufacturing and workforce development through its Appalachian Regional Initiative for Stronger Economies (ARISE) funding opportunity, which drives large-scale economic transformation through multi-state collaboration: <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li><strong>A $10 million ARISE grant to Catalyst Connection of Pittsburgh, PA <\/strong>will help 1,000+ small to medium manufacturers expand or enter emerging technology and energy sectors in 156 counties across Appalachian Pennsylvania, Maryland, New York, Ohio and West Virginia.  Funds will be used to provide training, technical assistance, supply chain mapping, and guidance on factory upgrades.<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li><strong>A $4.5+ million ARISE grant to Northern Tier Regional Planning and Development Commission of Towanda, PA <\/strong>will prepare and train an estimated 3,400+ students and young adults in Pennsylvania and New York for jobs in emerging industries. The project aims to engage employers with youth education providers to increase labor participation in Northern Appalachia. <\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cARC\u2019s ARISE grants are making transformational, cross-boundary impacts on our Appalachian states,\u201d said <strong>ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin<\/strong>. \u201cThe work of our newest ARISE grantees will help boost workforce opportunities across the region by providing integral job training in growing career fields, which in turn will allow Appalachia to compete economically on a national and global level.\u201d\u00a0<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The projects are funded through <a href=\"http:\/\/www.arc.gov\/ARISE\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">ARISE<\/a>, ARC\u2019s initiative to drive large-scale, regional economic transformation through multi-state collaborative projects across Appalachia. ARC launched ARISE in August of 2022 to strengthen Appalachian business and industry, and to grow and support the development of new economic opportunities across multiple states. A total of $227 million has been made available through ARISE to date.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cIn Kentucky, and across Appalachia, we\u2019re seeing the growth of new industries, bringing jobs an opportunity to our communities,\u201d said <strong>ARC 2023 States\u2019 Co-Chair, Kentucky Governor Andy Beshear<\/strong>. \u201cThese ARISE funds are helping ensure we have enough workers to meet the demands of this economic growth. This initiative is a great example of working across state lines to benefit our region and build a brighter future for all of our people.\u201d <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cPresident Biden\u2019s Investing in America agenda is growing the economy from the bottom up and middle out, bringing good-paying jobs to communities that have too long been overlooked,\u201d said <strong>Mitch Landrieu, Senior Advisor to the President and White House Infrastructure Coordinator<\/strong>. \u201cThese new ARISE awards will expand opportunity, boost domestic manufacturing, and bring the benefits of a clean energy economy to communities across Northern and Central Appalachia.\u201d\u00a0<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Learn more about ARISE and the recent updates to the application process: <a href=\"http:\/\/www.arc.gov\/ARISE\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">www.arc.gov\/ARISE<\/a>. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>About the Appalachian Regional Commission<\/strong><br>The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation.<\/p>\n<\/div>","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"<p>Two new ARISE grants strengthen manufacturing and energy production and prepare youth for in-demand jobs across five states. through its Appalachian Regional Initiative for Stronger Economies (ARISE) funding opportunity, which Announces Appalachian Leadership Institute Fellows for 2023-2024\u00a0"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong><em>New class of 40 fellows represents public, private, and nonprofit sectors across Appalachia\u2019s 13 states<\/em><\/strong> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>WASHINGTON, D.C., September 26, 2023<\/strong>\u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) announced the 40 fellows selected for the 2023-2024 Class of the <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/leadership\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Appalachian Leadership Institute<\/a>, a free leadership and economic development training opportunity for individuals currently living and\/or working in one of ARC\u2019s <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/appalachian-counties-served-by-arc\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">13 Appalachian states<\/a>. The 2023-2024 class comprises a diverse network of professionals representing all 13 Appalachian states and a wide spectrum of perspectives and sectors, including tourism, healthcare, education, civil service, and more.\u00a0\u00a0<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The nine-month curriculum is anchored by six multi-day seminars across the Appalachian Region focusing on each of ARC\u2019s <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/investment-priorities\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">strategic investment priorities<\/a>, which aim to strengthen economic and community growth in Appalachia. From October 2023 through July 2024, this class will focus on skill-building and best practice reviews to prepare fellows to: <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>Design effective economic development project proposals<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Integrate community assets into long-term economic development strategies<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Identify resources available to spark economic and community development<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Locate and access investment capital from a variety of public and private sources<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Prepare competitive applications for public grant opportunities<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Use expanded connections and leadership skills to create strong regional partnerships<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cGrowing and supporting the next generation of Appalachian leaders is integral to the future success of our region,\u201d said <strong>ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin<\/strong>. \u201cI send my sincerest congratulations to the newest round of Appalachian Leadership Institute fellows, as well as my appreciation for all the work and passion they put into helping their communities thrive and compete.\u201d <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The fifth class of Appalachian Leadership Institute fellows were selected via a competitive application process to reflect the Appalachian region\u2019s wide range of economic development challenges, opportunities, and strategies. The 2023-2024 Appalachian Leadership Institute fellows are:  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Alabama<\/strong> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li><strong>Kenneth Anderson<\/strong>, Director, City of Huntsville \u2013 DEI, Madison <\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li><strong>Paul Kennedy<\/strong>, President, Walker Area Community Foundation, Jasper <\/li>\n\n\n\n<li><strong>Jesslan Wilson<\/strong>, Director, Regional Planning Commission of Greater Birmingham, Trussville <\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Georgia<\/strong> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul automatically part of the Appalachian Leadership Institute Alumni Network, a peer-to-peer working group of experts helping foster collaborative solutions that will build a stronger future for Appalachia. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cThe Appalachian Leadership Institute supports leaders already doing amazing work across Eastern Kentucky and the entire Appalachian region,\u201d said <strong>ARC 2023 States\u2019 Co-Chair, Kentucky Governor Andy Beshear<\/strong>. \u201cThis program capitalizes on the incredibly talented people who are already invested in these special communities. When we invest in our people, we\u2019re building a brighter future for generations to come.\u201d <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">To learn more about the program, visit: <a href=\"http:\/\/www.arc.gov\/leadership\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">arc.gov\/leadership<\/a>. To meet the 2023-2024 class of fellows and learn more about how they are working to transform their Appalachian communities, <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/academies-and-institutes\/leadership\/class-of-2023-2024\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">visit this link<\/a>. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>About the Appalachian Leadership Institute<\/strong>  <br>The Appalachian Leadership Institute is a comprehensive regional leadership training program developed by the Appalachian Regional Commission in partnership with the University of Tennessee, Knoxville; The Howard H. Baker School of Public Policy and Public Affairs; Tuskegee University; and Collective Impact. Learn more at <a href=\"http:\/\/www.arc.gov\/leadership\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">www.arc.gov\/leadership<\/a>.  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>About the Appalachian Regional Commission<\/strong>  <br>The <a href=\"http:\/\/www.arc.gov\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Appalachian Regional Commission<\/a> is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the Region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation.
