Awards $6.3 Million to Bolster Broadband Access Across 50 Communities in Every Appalachian Subregion\u00a0"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>The first-ever grant funded through ARC\u2019s new Appalachian Regional Initiative for Stronger Economies (ARISE), led by Connect Humanity, will provide support to under-connected communities throughout Appalachia, better positioning them to grow broadband economic opportunities.<\/em><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--medium\">\n<figure class=\"wp-block-embed is-type-video is-provider-youtube wp-block-embed-youtube wp-embed-aspect-16-9 wp-has-aspect-ratio\"><div class=\"wp-block-embed__wrapper\">\n<iframe loading=\"lazy\" title=\"ARC Awards $6.3 Million to Bolster Broadband Access in Appalachia\" width=\"840\" height=\"473\" src=\"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/embed\/Y9SUYprEpoU?feature=oembed\" frameborder=\"0\" allow=\"accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture; web-share\" allowfullscreen><\/iframe>\n<\/div><\/figure>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>WASHINGTON, D.C., January 11, 2023<\/strong>\u2014Today, ARC awarded $6.3 million through its new <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/ARISE\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\"><strong>Appalachian Regional Initiative for Stronger Economies (ARISE)<\/strong><\/a> funding opportunity to <a href=\"https:\/\/connecthumanity.fund\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Connect Humanity<\/a> for a project that will help 50 underserved communities in <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/map\/subregions-in-appalachia\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">every subregion<\/a> of Appalachia plan for broadband access and growth.   <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Working with local partners in 12 Appalachian states\u2014Georgia, Kentucky, Maryland, Mississippi, New York, North Carolina, Ohio, Pennsylvania, South Carolina, Tennessee, Virginia, and West Virginia\u2014Connect Humanity will help communities develop tailored digital equity plans designed to deliver appropriate technology, business, and financing plans necessary to ensure high-speed and affordable internet for all, device access, and digital literacy training.\u202f The growing list of project partners include: <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"has-black-color has-text-color wp-block-list\"><li><a href=\"https:\/\/ruralinnovation.us\/our-work\/broadband\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Center on Rural Innovation<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/esd.ny.gov\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Empire State Development<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.theenterprisectr.org\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Enterprise Center<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.faircount.org\/technology-solutions\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Fair Count<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/generationwv.org\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Generation WV<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/alliancems.org\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Mississippi Alliance of Nonprofits & Philanthropy<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/iei.ncsu.edu\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">NC State University Institute for Emerging Issues<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/soar-ky.org\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">SOAR \u2013 Shaping Our Appalachian Region<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/studentfreedominitiative.org\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Student Freedom Initiative<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/dced.pa.gov\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Center for Strategic Partnerships at the Pennsylvania Department of Community & Economic Development<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/vafunders.org\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Virginia Funders Network<\/a> <\/li><\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">While a central focus of the project will be to help 50 under-connected communities across Appalachia compete for some of the $45 billion from the <a href=\"https:\/\/broadbandusa.ntia.doc.gov\/resources\/grant-programs\/broadband-equity-access-and-deployment-bead-program#:~:text=The%20Broadband%20Equity%2C%20Access%2C%20and,and%20the%20Commonwealth%20of%20the\">Broadband Equity, Access, and Deployment (BEAD)<\/a> and <a href=\"https:\/\/broadbandusa.ntia.doc.gov\/digital-equity-programs\">Digital Equity Act (DEA)<\/a> funding that will become available later in 2023, communities will also benefit from the technical assistance provided as part of the project to put them on a path to digital equity.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cARC\u2019s first ARISE grant to Connect Humanity has tremendous potential to drive large-scale, regional transformation around broadband access,\u201d said <strong>ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin<\/strong>. \u201cBroadband access is essential for Appalachia to thrive and compete in a global economy; without this support, our most rural communities may be left further behind. I commend our states and community partners in every Appalachian subregion for coming together in order to fully participate in our digitalized world.\u201d<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cPresident Biden\u2019s Bipartisan infrastructure Law is making sure we don\u2019t leave any community behind as we grow the economy from the bottom up and middle out, especially Appalachia,\u201d said <strong>Mitch Landrieu, Senior Advisor to the President and White House Infrastructure Coordinator.<\/strong> \u201cUnder the leadership of Co-Chair Gayle Manchin, ARC is providing critical resources to make sure states are prepared to help connect all of their residents to affordable, reliable high-speed internet.\u201d<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cAccess to affordable, high-speed internet is something every family across Appalachia needs and deserves. This project is another important step to ensuring broadband reaches every American,\u201d said<strong> ARC States\u2019 Co-Chair Governor Andy Beshear of Kentucky<\/strong>. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cWith the U.S. government about to make its biggest ever investment to close the digital divide, it\u2019s critical that the communities that most need support to build better broadband can secure these funds,\u201d said <strong>Connect Humanity Program Lead Chris Worman<\/strong>, who added, \u201cThis program will put 50 communities in Appalachia on the path to getting fast, reliable, affordable internet, serving hundreds of thousands of families and businesses with the connectivity they need to thrive in an increasingly digital world.\u201d <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Appalachian communities can learn more and apply at <a href=\"https:\/\/connecthumanity.fund\/arise\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">connecthumanity.fund\/arise<\/a>.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>ARC\u2019s ARISE Funding Opportunity<\/strong><br><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/ARISE\/\">ARISE<\/a> is ARC\u2019s newest initiative to drive large-scale, regional economic transformation through multi-state collaborative projects across Appalachia. Making available $73.5 million in FY 2022 provided through the Bipartisan Infrastructure Law, also known as the <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/news\/legislative-update-president-biden-signs-infrastructure-investment-and-jobs-act-which-includes-1-billion-for-arc-over-five-years\/\"><em>Infrastructure Investment and Jobs Act of 2021<\/em><\/a>, ARC launched ARISE in August of 2022 to strengthen Appalachian business and industry, and to grow and support the development of new economic opportunities across multiple states. Learn more: <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/ARISE\/\">arc.gov\/ARISE<\/a>.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em><strong>About the Appalachian Regional Commission<\/strong><\/em><br>The <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Appalachian Regional Commission<\/a> is an economic development agency of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC’s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the Region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation.<\/p>\n<\/div>","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"<p>The first-ever grant funded through ARC\u2019s new Appalachian Regional Initiative for Stronger Economies (ARISE), led by Connect Humanity, will provide support to under-connected communities throughout Appalachia, better positioning them to grow broadband economic opportunities. Regional Commission (ARC) Hosts 160 Appalachian College and University Students at Community Research Symposium\u00a0"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong><em>Beginning today, students from 16 colleges and universities in 10 Appalachian states will share capstone research at the 22<sup>nd<\/sup> annual Appalachian Teaching Project symposium. <\/em><\/strong><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>WASHINGTON, D.C., December 2, 2022<\/strong> \u2014 The Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) is hosting 160\u202fstudents from 16\u202fAppalachian colleges and universities for the 22<sup>nd<\/sup> annual <strong>Appalachian Teaching Project (ATP) <\/strong>Symposium at the Renaissance Arlington Capital View in Arlington, Virginia, today, Friday, December 2, and Saturday, December 3, 2022.   <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Supported by ARC and organized by the\u202fCenter for Appalachian Studies and Services\u202fat <a href=\"https:\/\/www.etsu.edu\/cas\/cass\/projects\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">East Tennessee State University (ETSU)<\/a>, participating schools offer a for-credit seminar during the fall semester to help students develop and execute field-based research projects that address the unique economic development needs of their communities and align with ARC\u2019s investment priorities. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">As a capstone, students will present their work to other student delegations from participating institutions, ARC leadership, and community leaders in a formal peer-to-peer conference setting hosted by ARC and the Consortium of Appalachian Colleges and Universities. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cYoung leaders, such as the students who participated in this year\u2019s Appalachian Teaching Project, are integral to shaping the future of economic development throughout Appalachia,\u201d said <strong>ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin<\/strong>. \u201cI\u2019m impressed by the hard work they put into their research projects and look forward to following their journeys as they continue to positively impact their communities, and communities across our region.\u201d <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Student delegations from the following schools participating in this year\u2019s ATP Symposium include:\u202f  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Alfred State College, <\/strong>Alfred, New York  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Appalachian State University<\/strong>,\u202fBoone, North Carolina\u202f  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Auburn University<\/strong>, Auburn, Alabama\u202f  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>East Tennessee State University<\/strong>, Johnson City, Tennessee\u202f  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Frostburg State University,<\/strong> Frostburg, Maryland  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Indiana University of Pennsylvania<\/strong>, Indiana, Pennsylvania\u202f  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Morehead State University<\/strong>, Morehead, Kentucky\u202f  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Muskingum University, <\/strong>New Concord, Ohio  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Ohio University<\/strong>, Athens, Ohio\u202f<strong> <\/strong>  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Radford University<\/strong>, Radford, Virginia\u202f  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>University of North Alabama<\/strong>, Florence, Alabama\u202f  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>University of Pittsburgh<\/strong>, Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania\u202f  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>University of Pittsburgh \u2013 Bradford<\/strong>, Bradford, Pennsylvania\u202f  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>University of Tennessee<\/strong>, Knoxville, Tennessee\u202f  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Virginia Tech,\u202f<\/strong>Blacksburg, Virginia\u202f  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>West Liberty University, <\/strong>West Liberty, West Virginia  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Summaries of each research project are available at\u202fon ARC\u2019s <a href=\"http:\/\/www.arc.gov\/ACRI\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\"><strong>website<\/strong><\/a><strong>.<\/strong> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Since 2001, more than\u202f2,800\u202fcollege and graduate students from 22 colleges and universities across Appalachia have participated in the Appalachian Teaching Project. During the symposium, ARC and ETSU will announce that ATP is being renamed to the <strong>Appalachian Collegiate Research Initiative (ACRI)<\/strong> to better reflect the valuable research participants prepare for communities. ARC has <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2022\/12\/2023-ACRI-RFP.pdf\">released an RFP<\/a> seeking college and university applicants for ACRI in 2023. <strong>Applications are due March 31, 2023.<\/strong> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">ACRI is one of ARC\u2019s four\u202f<a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/academies-and-institutes\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">academies and institutes,<\/a> offering experiential learning opportunities where participants build collaborative networks, hone skills, and foster an enduring commitment to Appalachia\u2019s future.\u202f  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>About the Appalachian Regional Commission<\/strong>  <br>The\u202f<a href=\"http:\/\/www.arc.gov\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Appalachian Regional Commission<\/a>\u202fis an economic development agency of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation. <\/p>\n<\/div>","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"<p>Beginning today, students from 16 colleges and universities in 10 Appalachian states will share capstone research at the 22nd Announces Appalachian Leadership Institute Fellows for 2022-2023"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong><em>New class of 40 fellows represents public, private, and nonprofit sectors across Appalachia\u2019s 13 states<\/em><\/strong><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>WASHINGTON, D.C., October 6, 2022<\/strong>\u2014Today the <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC)<\/a> announced the 40 fellows selected for the 2022-2023 Class of the <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/leadership\/\">Appalachian Leadership Institute<\/a>, a free leadership and economic development training opportunity for individuals currently living and\/or working in one of ARC\u2019s <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/appalachian-counties-served-by-arc\/\">13 Appalachian states<\/a>. The 2022-2023 class comprises a diverse network of professionals representing all 13 Appalachian states and a wide spectrum of perspectives and sectors, including tourism, healthcare, education, civil service, and more.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The nine-month curriculum is anchored by six multi-day seminars across the Appalachian region focusing on each of ARC\u2019s <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/strategicplan\/\">strategic investment priorities<\/a>, which aim to strengthen economic and community growth in Appalachia. The fourth class will run from October 2022 through July 2023, focusing on skill-building seminars and best practice reviews to prepare fellows to:<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>Design effective economic development project proposals<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Integrate community assets into long-term economic development strategies<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Identify resources available to spark economic and community development<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Locate and access investment capital from a variety of public and private sources<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Prepare competitive applications for public grant opportunities<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Use expanded connections and leadership skills to create strong regional partnerships<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">“Congratulations to the incoming 2022-2023 class of Appalachian Leadership Institute fellows! These leaders are already growing their Appalachian communities and will be even better equipped to drive positive change after their work with this program,\u201d said <strong>ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin<\/strong>. \u201cI am eager to see this class of fellows collaborate across state lines to set big goals that will help the entire Appalachian region thrive.\u201d<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Appalachian Leadership Institute fellows were selected via a competitive application process to reflect the Appalachian region\u2019s wide range of economic development challenges, opportunities, and strategies. The 2022-2023 Appalachian Leadership Institute fellows are:<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Alabama<\/strong><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li><strong>Gary Padgett, <\/strong>Professor, University of North Alabama, Florence<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li><strong>Jenny Stubbs, <\/strong>Owner, Yellow House Publishing, Wetumpka<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain Georgia, Dahlonega<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Kentucky<\/strong><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain Baker Center for Public Policy; Tuskegee University; and Collective Impact. Regional Commission to Launch Virtual \u201cRoadshow\u201d Series to Showcase Impact of Strategic Investments in Appalachian Communities"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>Launching March 1, the Virtual Event Series Will Feature Governors, Community Members, and Economic Development Professionals Highlighting Workforce Development<\/em><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>WASHINGTON, D.C., February 10, 2022<\/strong>\u2014Following the reveal of its new 2022\u20132026 strategic plan, \u201cAppalachia Envisioned,\u201d the <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC)<\/a> will embark on a virtual event series to highlight how ARC\u2019s investments are transforming Appalachian communities in each of its 13 states. The virtual event series (titled the <em>Appalachia Envisioned Roadshow<\/em>) will showcase the voices and experiences of Appalachian communities leveraging ARC grant funding to strengthen and fuel economic growth in the region.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Each roadshow event will spotlight one of ARC\u2019s five strategic investment priorities and feature voices of governors, community members, and economic development professionals from ARC partner states. <em>Stop 1: Building Workforce Ecosystems in Maryland, New York and Pennsylvania<\/em>, the first event of the series, will take place on <strong>Tuesday, March 1<\/strong> from<strong>9:30-11:30 am EST<\/strong>. ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin will facilitate an engaging discussion with ARC States\u2019 Co-Chair Governor Larry Hogan of Maryland on the challenges and successes in creating and expanding workforce ecosystems in his state. Community members will also participate in a panel to share their experiences from the frontlines of bringing ARC\u2019s investment priority of building workforce ecosystems to life.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cThe future of Appalachia is bright because of the individuals in the region actively engaged in efforts to strengthen and elevate their communities each day,\u201d said <strong>ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin<\/strong>. \u201cThe <em>Appalachia Envisioned Roadshow<\/em> is a five-part virtual event series, which will spotlight the voices and experiences of leaders and communities in all 13 states, while facilitating conversations about resilience, innovation, sustainability, and equity through the lens of ARC\u2019s new strategic plan. I look forward to kicking off the series with our partners in Maryland, Pennsylvania, and New York, as well as collaborating with additional state partners for future events.\u201d<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cEach day, communities across Appalachia are transformed through investments made by the ARC,\u201d said <strong>ARC States\u2019 Co-Chair Governor Larry Hogan.<\/strong> \u201cThere is no better way to further amplify and strengthen our mission than to spotlight the success stories of leaders and communities throughout the region.\u201d<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Dates and times are forthcoming for the remaining four roadshow events, which will focus on the following ARC strategic investment priorities:<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\">\n<li>Building Appalachian Business<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Building Appalachia’s Infrastructure<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Building Regional Culture and Tourism<\/li>\n\n\n\n<li>Building Community Leaders and Capacity <\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Learn more and register for <em>Stop #1: Building Workforce Ecosystems with Maryland, New York, and Pennsylvania.<\/em> To receive the latest information about the remaining <em>Appalachia Envisioned Roadshow<\/em> stops, sign up for ARC\u2019s <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/newsletter-signup\/\"><em>In the Region <\/em>newsletter<\/a>.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--medium\">\n<figure class=\"wp-block-embed is-type-video is-provider-youtube wp-block-embed-youtube wp-embed-aspect-16-9 wp-has-aspect-ratio\"><div class=\"wp-block-embed__wrapper\">\n<iframe loading=\"lazy\" title=\"ARC's Appalachia Envisioned Roadshow\" width=\"840\" height=\"473\" src=\"https:\/\/www.youtube.com\/embed\/_P40A5f70nQ?feature=oembed\" frameborder=\"0\" allow=\"accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture\" allowfullscreen><\/iframe>\n<\/div><\/figure>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>About the Appalachian Regional Commission<\/strong> <br>The <a href=\"http:\/\/www.arc.gov\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Appalachian Regional Commission<\/a> is an economic development agency of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the Region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation.  <\/p>\n<\/div>","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"<p>Launching March 1, the Virtual Event Series Will Feature Governors, Community Members, and Economic Development Professionals Commission (ARC) will embark on a virtual event series to highlight how ARC\u2019s investments are transforming Appalachian communities Hosts 21st Annual Appalachian Teaching Project (ATP) Featuring Economic Development Research Conducted by Region\u2019s College and University Students"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>150 Students from 15 Colleges and Universities in 10 Appalachian States to Participate in Online Symposium<\/em> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>WASHINGTON, D.C.<\/strong>, <strong>November 30, 2021<\/strong>\u2014This week, from Tuesday, November 30 to Friday, December 3, 2021, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) will host 150 students from 15 Appalachian based colleges and universities at the 21st annual\u202f<a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/atp\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Appalachian Teaching Project (ATP) Conference<\/a>.  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">ATP is an applied-research academic program training college and graduate students to design community based economic development initiatives across the Appalachian Region. As part of ATP, students enroll in a for-credit course and conduct field research to identify and solve a community development issue during the fall semester. As a capstone to this work, students present their work to other student delegations from ATP participating institutions, ARC leadership, and community leaders in a formal peer-to-peer conference setting hosted by ARC. Normally, this conference is held in Washington, D.C. where students use the trip as an opportunity to meet with policy leaders and learn more about the city. However, due to the COVID-19 pandemic, the ATP Symposium is being held online again this year. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cAppalachia needs young leaders, like those participating in the Appalachian Teaching Project, to bring forward-thinking economic development plans to life,\u201d said <strong>ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin<\/strong>. \u201cThis group of change-makers is capable of creating positive impacts in our communities, and I am excited to see the lasting effects these innovative projects will have on the Region long-term.\u201d\u00a0<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Student delegations from the following schools participating in this year\u2019s ATP will give presentations focusing on addressing community needs and challenges, economic development, tourism, and capacity building: <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Alfred State College<\/strong>, Alfred, New York <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Appalachian State University<\/strong>, Boone, North Carolina <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Auburn University<\/strong>, Auburn, Alabama <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>East Tennessee State University<\/strong>, Johnson City, Tennessee <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Fairmont State University<\/strong>, Fairmont, West Virginia <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Indiana University of Pennsylvania<\/strong>, Indiana, Pennsylvania <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Ohio University<\/strong>, Athens, Ohio <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Morehead State University<\/strong>, Morehead, Kentucky <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Radford University<\/strong>, Radford, Virginia <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>University of North Alabama<\/strong>, Florence, Alabama <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>University of Pittsburgh<\/strong>, Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>University of Pittsburgh – Bradford<\/strong>, Bradford, Pennsylvania <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>University of Tennessee<\/strong>, Knoxville, Tennessee <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Virginia Tech, <\/strong>Blacksburg, Virginia <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Young Harris College<\/strong>, Young Harris, Georgia <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Since 2001, over 2,650 college and graduate students from across Appalachia have participated in the Appalachian Teaching Project. Many ATP alumnae have gone on to careers in public service, community development, and other related fields across the Region. Summaries of each research project are available at <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/appalachian-teaching-project\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">www.arc.gov\/atp<\/a>. ATP is one of four <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/academies-and-institutes\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">academies and institutes<\/a> sponsored by the Appalachian Regional Commission. Through these experiential learning opportunities, participants build networks, hone skills, and cultivate an enduring commitment to Appalachia\u2019s future. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Supported by ARC and organized by the\u202f<a href=\"http:\/\/www.etsu.edu\/cas\/cass\/projects\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Center for Appalachian Studies and Services<\/a>\u202fat East Tennessee State University (ETSU), students receive college credit for participating in ATP, and schools participating in ATP offer a directed seminar guiding students in developing and executing field-based research projects specific to the needs of their surrounding communities and in alignment with ARC\u2019s <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/investment-priorities\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">investment priorities<\/a>. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>About the Appalachian Regional Commission<\/strong> <br>The <a href=\"http:\/\/www.arc.gov\">Appalachian Regional Commission<\/a> is an economic development agency of the federal government and 13 state<strong> <\/strong>governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the Region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation. <\/p>\n<\/div>","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"<p>150 Students from 15 Colleges and Universities in 10 Appalachian States to Participate in Online Symposium  WASHINGTON, universities at the 21st annual\u202fAppalachian Teaching Project (ATP) Conference.   ATP is an applied-research academic program training college and Announces Appalachian Leadership Institute Fellows For 2021-2022"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u202f<em>Class Includes 40 Fellows Representing Variety of Disciplines and Backgrounds from 13 Appalachian States<\/em>\u202f <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u202f<strong>WASHINGTON, D.C., September 28, 2021<\/strong>\u2014Today, the\u202f<a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Appalachian Regional Commission<\/a>\u202f(ARC) announced 40 Fellows who will be participating in the third class of the\u202f<a href=\"https:\/\/arc.gov\/leadership\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">Appalachian Leadership Institute<\/a>. The class represents Appalachia\u2019s 13 states and includes professionals who live and\/or work in the Region in a variety of sectors including education, healthcare, economic development, tourism, and others. \u202f\u202f <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">As a part of the Leadership Institute, each Fellow will participate in an extensive,\u202fnine-month series of skill-building seminars featuring regional experts, peer-to-peer learning, and case study analysis. The program begins\u202fvirtually\u202fin October and culminates with the Class of 2021-2022’s graduation in July 2022.\u202f  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Participation in this program will empower Fellows to take action toward positively impacting the future of Appalachia with collaborative solutions to our Region\u2019s greatest challenges. Fellows will learn to: <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\"><li>Identify and implement best practices and strategies to inspire positive change in their communities; <\/li><li>Build a robust network of leaders across the Region; <\/li><li>Integrate community assets into long-lasting economic development strategies; and  <\/li><li>Appreciate the diversity and commonalties of the 13 states in the Appalachian Region.   <\/li><\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cI am honored to congratulate the 2021-2022 class of Appalachian Leadership Institute fellows. These phenomenal Appalachians are the driving force of change in their communities,\u201d said <strong>ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin<\/strong>. \u201cTheir perseverance and dedication to the region is evident, and I have no doubt that this program will aid them in continuing to help their communities grow and thrive. The work of this class of fellows\u2014and the ones who came before– gives me hope to the future of our Region and is a testament to all that can be accomplished when Appalachians work together.\u201d <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Upon completion of the program, Appalachian Leadership Institute Fellows will automatically become part of the Appalachian Leadership Institute Alumni network, a peer-to-peer working group committed to Appalachia\u2019s future.\u202f <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u202f”The Appalachian Leadership Institute offers an incredible opportunity for fellows to learn firsthand from community leaders and develop crucial skills,” said <strong>ARC States’ Co-Chair Governor Ralph Northam<\/strong>. “The Institute helps build strong, local leaders who will make their communities more resilient and ensure the economic vitality of the Appalachian Region for years to come.” <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Appalachian Leadership Institute Fellows were chosen via a competitive application process. More than 115 qualified applications were received for this year\u2019s program. The 2021-2022 Appalachian Leadership Institute Fellows are:\u202f <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p Baker Center for Public Policy; Tuskegee University; and Collective Impact, LLC.  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>About the Appalachian Regional Commission<\/strong><br>The <a href=\"http:\/\/www.arc.gov\/\">Appalachian Regional Commission<\/a> is an economic development agency of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 420 counties across the Appalachian Region. pi.w.org\/{rel}","templated":true}]}},{"id":26612,"date":"2021-02-10T13:00:43","date_gmt":"2021-02-10T18:00:43","guid":{"rendered":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/?p=26612"},"modified":"2021-03-11T14:11:28","modified_gmt":"2021-03-11T19:11:28","slug":"extending-our-welcome-trends-and-strategies-for-tourism-in-appalachia","status":"publish","type":"post","link":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/news\/extending-our-welcome-trends-and-strategies-for-tourism-in-appalachia\/","title":{"rendered":"New Research Identifies Opportunities and Challenges in Appalachia’s Tourism Economy"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong><em>Recommendations and Twelve Community Case Studies Available for Use in Local Tourism Planning<\/em><\/strong> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>WASHINGTON, D.C.,\u00a0<\/strong>February 10, 2021 \u2013\u00a0Today, Appalachian Regional Commission released\u202f<em><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/report\/extending-our-welcome-trends-and-strategies-for-tourism-in-appalachia\/\">Extending Our Welcome: Trends and Strategies for Tourism in Appalachia<\/a><\/em>, a new report produced in partnership with\u00a0University of Tennessee Knoxville\u00a0and\u00a0Collective Impact. Using qualitative and quantitative research, including publicly available data,\u00a0surveys administered as part of the research,\u00a0and\u00a0on-site visits,\u00a0the report looks at the history of tourism in Appalachia and makes recommendations for communities looking to employ it as an economic strategy.\u00a0Though the research was conducted prior to COVID-19, many of these findings remain relevant\u2014bearing in mind the pandemic\u2019s impact on the tourism industry and public safety guidelines\u2014and\u00a0useful for current and future tourism planning.\u00a0<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>Extending Our Welcome<\/em>\u202ffound that most of Appalachia\u2019s 420 counties experience modest levels of tourism, and often struggle to strengthen this sector due to lack of capacity and resources. To help those areas better establish tourism infrastructure and support more vibrant asset-based economies, the report makes several recommendations, including:\u202f <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\"><li>Embracing major changes in modern tourism, including the role played by technology and the diversity of the current traveling public,\u202f <\/li><li>Educating and training local officials to help them better understand local tourism strategies,\u202f <\/li><li>Connecting communities, especially rural areas, with hands-on technical assistance in tourism planning and hospitality,\u202f <\/li><li>Training rural communities to prepare for and welcome visitors of all backgrounds, <\/li><\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\"><li>Practicing good stewardship of public lands, which are the foundation of Appalachian tourism,\u202f <\/li><li>Creating authentic local experiences unique to a community’s natural and cultural assets, and\u202f <\/li><li>Addressing issues, like substance abuse, affecting Appalachia’s front line tourism workforce.\u202f <\/li><\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">“Tourism has an important role in successful community economic development strategies in the Appalachian Region and will continue to do so,\u201d said <strong>ARC Federal Co-Chairman Tim Thomas<\/strong>. \u201cOur Region has much to offer families seeking leisure and recreation, and this important research highlights the challenges and opportunities for Appalachian communities seeking to build their tourism capacity.” <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">In addition to providing recommendations, which are rooted in survey responses from over 500 visitors to Appalachia and more than 700 regional tourism stakeholders, the report also profiles 12 communities and establishments across eight states as a source of best practices in Appalachia’s current tourism landscape. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\"><li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/02\/ARC-Tourism-report-final-Dec-2020-1.pdf#page=56\">Red Bay, Alabama<\/a><\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/02\/ARC-Tourism-report-final-Dec-2020-1.pdf#page=92\">Pikeville, Kentucky<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/02\/ARC-Tourism-report-final-Dec-2020-1.pdf#page=148\">Berea, Kentucky<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/02\/ARC-Tourism-report-final-Dec-2020-1.pdf#page=152\">Iuka, Mississippi and the Natchez Trace Parkway<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/02\/ARC-Tourism-report-final-Dec-2020-1.pdf#page=190\">Jamestown, New York<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/02\/ARC-Tourism-report-final-Dec-2020-1.pdf#page=168\">Boone, North Carolina<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/02\/ARC-Tourism-report-final-Dec-2020-1.pdf#page=134\">Coudersport, Pennsylvania<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/02\/ARC-Tourism-report-final-Dec-2020-1.pdf#page=172\">St. Marys, Pennsylvania<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/02\/ARC-Tourism-report-final-Dec-2020-1.pdf#page=114\">Marietta, Ohio<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/02\/ARC-Tourism-report-final-Dec-2020-1.pdf#page=52\">Erwin, Tennessee<\/a> <\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/02\/ARC-Tourism-report-final-Dec-2020-1.pdf#page=70\">Point Pleasant, West Virginia<\/a><\/li><li><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/02\/ARC-Tourism-report-final-Dec-2020-1.pdf#page=194\">Williamson, West Virginia<\/a> <\/li><\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">“Now more than ever, Appalachian communities have an important opportunity to extend their welcome to visitors,” said <strong>Dr. Tim <\/strong><strong>Ezzell<\/strong><strong> of University of Tennessee Knoxville<\/strong>, who led the university’s research efforts for this report. “As COVID subsides, Appalachia’s assets – public lands, outdoor recreation, clean open spaces, and welcoming, authentic communities – offer safe and convenient opportunities for Americans looking to travel again. We hope the lessons and ideas in this report can help Appalachian communities leverage pent-up demand for travel and create opportunities for economic recovery and growth.” <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>Extending Our Welcome<\/em> was conducted by a team from the University of Tennessee Knoxville and Collective Impact, with support and guidance from the Appalachian Regional Commission.  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>About the Appalachian Regional Commission<\/strong>\u202f <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The Appalachian Regional Commission (<a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\">www.arc.gov<\/a>) is an economic development agency of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 420 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the Region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation. <\/p>\n<\/div>","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"<p>Recommendations and Twelve Community Case Studies Available for Use in Local Tourism Planning  WASHINGTON, D.C.,\u00a0February 10, 2021 \u2013\u00a0Today, Appalachian Regional Commission released\u202fExtending Our Welcome: Trends and Strategies for Tourism in Appalachia, a new report produced in partnership with\u00a0University of Tennessee Knoxville\u00a0and\u00a0Collective Impact. Governor Ralph Northam Named 2021 Appalachian Regional Commission States\u2019 Co-Chair"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><em>ARC\u2019s unique federal-state partnership model invested more than $160 million in Appalachia\u2019s economic development during Fiscal Year 2020 <\/em><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>WASHINGTON, D.C.,\u00a0<\/strong>February 3, 2021\u2014\u00a0Virginia\u00a0Governor Ralph Northam has been selected to serve as the Appalachian Regional Commission\u2019s States\u2019\u00a0Co-chair for 2021. As part of ARC\u2019s\u00a0unique\u00a0federal-state partnership structure, the states\u2019 co-chair works with the federal\u00a0office of the ARC\u00a0and fellow\u00a0Appalachian state\u00a0governors\u00a0to invest in economic growth and development across the\u00a0Region.\u00a0\u00a0<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Governor Northam is ARC\u2019s 68th states\u2019 co-chair. He is the first states\u2019 co-chair from Virginia since 2004 and is the fifth Governor of Virginia to serve in this role. He is also the first retired Army officer to serve in this position in 24 years.<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">\u201cIt\u2019s an honor to be elected to this role, and I look forward to working with leaders across the Appalachian region to advance our shared priorities and support the region’s needs,\u201d said <strong>Virginia Governor Ralph Northam<\/strong>. \u201cAppalachia is a vital part of Virginia, and America, with a unique history and culture, beautiful landscapes, and resilient people. I am eager to work with the ARC to strengthen Appalachian communities, improving their economic health and livelihood through investments in critical infrastructure like broadband, and services like health care, especially in the fight against opioid addiction. By exchanging new ideas, from both sides of the aisle, we can bring high quality jobs and economic opportunity throughout the Appalachian region.”<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">“I am pleased to welcome Governor Northam as ARC’s 2021 States’ Co-Chair,” said <strong>ARC’s Executive Director Brandon McBride<\/strong>. \u201cOn behalf of the Commission, I want to thank Ohio Governor Mike DeWine for serving in this role in 2020. ARC’s federal-state partnership is critical to the fulfillment of our important mission, and I look forward to working with Governor Northam, and all Appalachian state governors, to continue strengthening the Region.”<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Since 1965, ARC has been committed to continued economic development across the Appalachian region, which spans 420 counties in 13 states from southern New York to northern Mississippi, and is home to over 25 million people. ARC\u2019s investments are made in partnership with each of the Region\u2019s 13 governors.  During FY 2020, ARC invested <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/02\/FY2020PerformanceAndAccountabilityReport.pdf\">$161.8 million<\/a> in the Region, which is projected to leverage an additional $858 million in private investments, create or retain over 26,000 jobs and train nearly 21,000 students, workers, and leaders in new skills across the Appalachian region. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Learn more about ARC\u2019s impact in each of <a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/appalachian-states\/\">the Region\u2019s 13 states<\/a>: <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/01\/ALABAMA_FY2020.pdf\">Alabama <\/a><\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/01\/GEORGIA_FY2020.pdf\">Georgia<\/a> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/01\/KENTUCKY_FY2020.pdf\">Kentucky<\/a> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/01\/MARYLAND_FY2020.pdf\">Maryland<\/a> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/01\/MISSISSIPPI_FY2020.pdf\">Mississippi<\/a> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/01\/NEW-YORK_FY2020.pdf\">New York<\/a> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/01\/NORTH-CAROLINA_FY2020.pdf\">North Carolina<\/a> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/01\/OHIO_FY2020.pdf\">Ohio<\/a> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/01\/PENNSYLVANIA_FY2020.pdf\">Pennsylvania<\/a> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/wp-content\/uploads\/2021\/01\/SOUTH-CAROLINA_FY2020.pdf\">South l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>About the Appalachian Regional Commission<\/strong> <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The Appalachian Regional Commission (<a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/\">www.arc.gov<\/a>) is an economic development agency of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 420 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the Region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation.<\/p>\n<\/div>","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"<p>ARC\u2019s unique federal-state partnership model invested more than $160 million in Appalachia\u2019s economic development during Fiscal Year 2020  WASHINGTON, D.C.,\u00a0February 3, 2021\u2014\u00a0Virginia\u00a0Governor Ralph Northam has been selected to serve as the Appalachian Regional Commission\u2019s States\u2019\u00a0Co-chair for 2021. state\u00a0governors\u00a0to invest in economic pi.w.org\/{rel}","templated":true}]}},{"id":25391,"date":"2020-11-19T11:00:39","date_gmt":"2020-11-19T16:00:39","guid":{"rendered":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/?p=25391"},"modified":"2021-03-11T14:13:20","modified_gmt":"2021-03-11T19:13:20","slug":"research-finds-that-appalachias-diseases-of-despair-mortality-rates-continue-at-disproportionately-higher-rates-in-comparison-to-rest-of-nation","status":"publish","type":"post","link":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/news\/research-finds-that-appalachias-diseases-of-despair-mortality-rates-continue-at-disproportionately-higher-rates-in-comparison-to-rest-of-nation\/","title":{"rendered":"Research Finds That Appalachia\u2019s Diseases of Despair Mortality Rates Continue at Disproportionately Higher Rates in Comparison to Rest of Nation"},"content":{"rendered":"<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<h2 class=\"wp-block-heading\">For Women, Disease of Despair Mortality Rates  in Appalachia  Over 45 Percent Higher Than Rest of Country <\/h2>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><strong>Washington, D.C.,<\/strong> November, 19, 2020 —\u00a0\u00a0<em><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/report\/appalachian-diseases-of-despair\/\">Appalachian Diseases of Despair<\/a><\/em>, a new report from the\u00a0<a rel=\"noreferrer noopener\" href=\"http:\/\/www.arc.gov\/\" target=\"_blank\">Appalachian Regional Commission<\/a> finds that despite overall declining mortality rates from diseases of despair (overdose, suicide, and liver disease) between 2017-2018, the Region\u2019s diseases of despair mortality rate in 2018 was still 36 percent higher than the rate for the non-Appalachian United States. Moreover,\u00a0among Appalachians in the prime working ages of 25\u201354, the diseases of despair mortality rate\u00a0was\u00a043 percent higher in the Region than the rest of the country.\u00a0The\u00a0report also found that for\u00a0men\u00a0ages 15-64, the\u00a0diseases of despair mortality rate was\u00a031\u00a0percent higher in\u00a0the Appalachian Region than in the rest of the country;\u00a0for women, however, the disparity was even larger,\u00a0with\u00a0the\u00a0rate\u00a046\u00a0percent higher in the Region\u00a0than in the non-Appalachian United States.\u00a0\u00a0<\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"> \u201cWhile  trends revealed by this report may seem positive, the Appalachian Region still needs support,” said ARC Federal Co-Chairman Tim Thomas. “Researchers and  policy makers rely on this important data to explore solutions to the issues we face. This report highlights why ARC\u2019s economic development efforts are so critical when it comes to addressing issues like substance abuse.\u201d <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Additionally, the report, which was released today as part of National Rural Health Day, found that in 2018: <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\"><li>West Virginia and Appalachian Maryland had the highest diseases of despair mortality rates among all Appalachian states, while the Appalachian portions of Mississippi, Georgia, and New York had the lowest. In Appalachian Maryland, West Virginia, Appalachian Ohio, and Appalachian Pennsylvania, at least 60 percent of diseases of despair deaths were due to overdose. <\/li><\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\"><li>The states with the highest percentages of overdose deaths attributed to opioids within their Appalachian portions were Maryland (92 percent); West Virginia (83 percent); North Carolina (81 percent); Ohio (78 percent); and Virginia (76 percent). The states with the lowest percentage of overdose deaths attributed to opioids were Appalachian Mississippi (45 percent) and Appalachian Alabama (54 percent). <\/li><\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<ul class=\"wp-block-list\"><li>While the rate of overdose deaths was greater in metro counties in Appalachia, the rates of suicide and<s> <\/s> liver disease<s> <\/s> were higher in nonmetro counties. <\/li><\/ul>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">Anticipating the impact the COVID crisis and other events may have on future findings, the report notes that \u201cAnecdotal evidence has suggested dramatic increases in overdose deaths in many regions during the COVID-19 pandemic. The impact of COVID-19 will likely lead to an increase in mortality from diseases of despair, particularly as the Appalachian Region and the rest of the United States experience economic challenges as a result of the pandemic, isolation, and limitations on access to in-person treatment and recovery support. It will be important to continue to monitor these trends in diseases of despair mortality in the Appalachian Region.\u201d <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\"><a href=\"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/report\/appalachian-diseases-of-despair\/\">Appalachian Diseases of Despair<\/a> research was conducted by The Walsh Center for Rural Health Analysis at NORC at the University of Chicago, and the Center for Rural Health Research at East Tennessee State University, and draws on 2018 mortality data from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. The report is a follow up to similar research  released in 2017 when the United States was seeing a dramatic rise in overdose deaths from synthetic opioids, particularly those involving illicitly manufactured fentanyl.   <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">In 2019, ARC established the Substance Abuse Advisory Council (SAAC)  to address the disproportionate impact substance abuse continues to have on the Region\u2019s workforce in comparison to the rest of the country. Earlier this year, ARC launched the INvestments Supporting Partnerships In Recovery Ecosystems (INSPIRE) Initiative, a $10 million initiative addressing the substance abuse crisis by creating or expanding a recovery ecosystem that will lead to workforce entry or re-entry. ARC received more than  60 applications in response to INSPIRE\u2019s inaugural request for proposals. Grant announcement are expected in early 2021. <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">About the Appalachian Regional Commission  <\/p>\n<\/div>\n\n<div class=\"l-constrain l-constrain--small\">\n<p class=\"wp-block-paragraph\">The Appalachian Regional Commission (www.arc.gov) is an economic development agency of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 420 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the Region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation. <\/p>\n<\/div>","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":"<p>For Women, Disease of Despair Mortality Rates  in Appalachia  Over 45 Percent Higher Than Rest of Country  Washington, D.C.,
